[[Assentment.]] Debate taken into account by the G.P. of the Socialists of Galicia, amending Law 13/2008, of 3 December, of social services of Galicia, for the introduction and promotion of social concerts
ParlaMint-ES-GA_2015-09-15-DSPG120.u12	Mrs Gallego Calvar speaks for the Socialist Parliamentary Group.
ParlaMint-ES-GA_2015-09-15-DSPG120.u13	Thank you very much, Madam President. Ladies and gentlemen, good morning. Good morning, too, and welcome to all the representatives of the social initiative institutions that accompany us today, social and non-profit institutions that work in all the social and civil life in our country and who are invited here today by the Socialist Parliamentary Group to attend the debate of this bill that we present. It's a pity we can't welcome the President of the Union this morning too, the President who is absent, that president who spends the weekends going through Spain and going through television dishes instead of dealing with Galician problems. That is certainly why you cannot come to this Parliament today either to discuss the problems of the dairy sector or to tell us why the citizens of southern Galicia have a second public health rather than having the same rights to health as the rest of the citizens. Probably because he's very busy campaigning in other places instead of being in the Galician Parliament and not in Galicia dealing with the problems of the Greeks and the Greeks. The Socialist Parliamentary Group today presents this bill that it is now doing to amend Law 13/2008, the Galician Social Services Act, basically in Title II and Articles 29 and 30, those that speak of the form of provision of social services in our autonomous community. As your lordships know, as we all know, social services can be provided directly from the public administration itself, or indirectly. And in the case of the indirect way because they can be provided through private, commercial, business or private but non-profit entities, which we want to refer to on this initiative. This proposed law that we present today for consideration – this is therefore the initial debate – consists of 9 articles, a transitional provision, a derogation provision and two final provisions. It concerns, of course, the autonomous, state and Community regulatory framework, and essentially it is intended to guarantee with more legal force the priority of social and non-profit initiative entities in the case of contracts and concessions of all kinds from public administrations to commercial entities. We also seek with this proposal a greater recognition of the role played by social initiative entities, third sector entities in the autonomous community. On Friday last week we attended many of those we are here on the 25th anniversary of Cogami, one of the largest and most important organizations in the sector working with people with disabilities in Galicia. There was talk, on that 25th anniversary, about how Cogami's beginnings were, about how families were joining together to work for people who had some kind of functional diversity, and how those little families were building organizations that today are pointers in the field of labour insertion, in the field of social inclusion, and are ultimately points, in giving them visibility to people with some kind of disability, and in demonstrating that they are also equal in their ability to people who do not have any kind of disability. That recognition was made on this 25th anniversary of Cogami, the socialists want to do it for the whole social and non-profit entity that is here today and for those who could not come too, because we understand that they were the ones who pushed the various administrations to seek and obtain new rights for people – the right to health, the right to education, in this case the right to social services –. It was they who were the point of launch, who were the pioneers in that struggle of integration, and without them it is impossible to understand, today, the coverage that can be given to people with functional diversity, because in many cases the administrations themselves went to third sector organizations and the means of third sector organizations to address the deficiencies that the administrations had. The socialists understand that it is the public administrations who have to guarantee a ratio of services and public squares to care for people, to meet people's rights. In the absence of the possibility of meeting all the people who demand these services, we understand that complementarity must be sought, and that complementarity must be exercised from the administration together with social initiative entities, third sector entities, and not commercial entities. Because it's about providing services to guarantee people's rights, not providing services to guarantee companies' benefits. And so when we talk about rights, when we talk about people, we understand that we have to guarantee these rights to those people with quality, always looking for the best comfort of people and not commercial performance. That is why we are presenting this initiative, an initiative which wishes to support the unanimity of this plenary, this Parliament. Because what we're starting today is the first step in this bill. We want to count on the support of all the House's groups, also counting on the fact that our proposal can be improved and can be enriched in the presentation debate so that we can then have an amendment to that Social Services Act that serves all of us, third sector entities, but also the whole population of Galicia. It is true that, with political will, with the current Social Services Act of 2008, if we look at what it says in its articles 29, 30 et seq., we could force that in certain situations, for example for companies with disabled workers over 2%, or for social initiative entities dedicated to the social and labour integration of persons in social exclusion, these entities could be prioritized, in this case before a contract, of competitive competition. But for the reasons that we all know, the truth is that in recent years only three of the centers that were opened by the Public Service, of any kind, were residences and older people, were childhood schools, were day centers, etc., etc., only three were directly managed by the Administration itself; the others, they all went out. And of those who have outsourced themselves, an important part of this outsourcing was commercial enterprises, that is, private companies, who clearly seek because business profit and not the guarantee of rights. It is also true that in these outsourcings of these private companies four companies are constantly repeated throughout Galicia; that is, there are four companies operating in this sector and they are often winning competitive competitions, which is a serious problem for third sector entities, social-initiative entities. We have the most recent example, which is the case of San Francisco, the case of the Vigo center, which was won this contest by Doral Residences, S.L., one of the companies that appears continuously in the resolution of these contests, this case, which, as we have seen all of us are here when we brought it to Parliament, because I have put a serious problem for families who had children and children and people who entered that center, in downtown San Francisco, with the upheaval that involves moving them from the Town Hall, to their usual surroundings. But that's not the only case, because the truth is, in the face of a public competition, in the face of the competitive competition on equal terms, an entity in the third sector that is working, like Cogami, for example, twenty-five years old and has centres, that is managing centers with templates because they've been working for more than twenty years, they can't compete in labour costs with a private company that has just hired workers, they have workers with precarious contracts. And besides, it's not just that you can't, it's that you don't, because we understand that these public services must be given in quality and that the workers and workers who offer these public services must also have good working conditions. So, in free competitive competition, without any kind of support for third sector entities or social initiative, what we're doing is leaving them out of the way, what we're doing is we're leaving these social-initiative entities and picking up commercial entities because they're the ones that can compete better at prices, so we understand that this change is necessary. It is necessary, firstly, because we already have a Community directive of the year 2014, which is pending the transposition of state legislation, which tells us that social concerts can be held in the case, for example, of social services, and that these concerts can be held separately from the rest of the concerts. We therefore have Community rules which protect us and which are already in force; regardless of whether the Government of Spain has yet to transpose State legislation, this Community directive is already in force. What does this Socialist Group initiative say? It allows, on the one hand, to regulate the regime of this social concert to carry it out through private and social initiative entities. It establishes this differentiated scheme of the general concert which regulates the general public sector procurement rules; precisely because of this specificity of social services and to prioritize these entities in the third sector. It also looks for this bill in concerts for a social clause that sets out the principles of personalized attention, the roots of people in the environment, continuity and quality in care; that is, principles that guarantee better provision of services and rights that provide social services. On the other hand, as I said, what this bill makes is to recognize the role of the third sector with the public funding of social initiative entities, not only through concerts, but also by laying down other formulas for the maintenance agreement, for the maintenance of the daily activity of third sector entities. It is also established – as I said at the beginning of my intervention – a transitional provision which aims – if this bill is approved – to maintain the current concerts, current agreements, contracts, concerts, which are already signed until the law is developed; precisely to prevent the change of legislation from producing those roots and changes of centres that I was talking about before, which may affect families and people who are in those centers. Obviously, as I said at the outset, what we are discussing today is the consideration of this bill, for what I call for the favourable vote of all parliamentary groups – I would like to say – in the spirit that, if taken into consideration, we can, among all, be enriched, bring new ideas which, while respecting the current regulatory framework, can reinforce the idea that we are moving to socialists with their presentation: social services are public policies that guarantee subjective rights of persons, equal to health rights or education, which must always be provided from the public with quality, and in addition to the entities of the third sector, to social initiative entities. I think that principle should touch us all and everyone enough so that all your lords can vote in favour of taking into account this bill, which is neither original nor the first, because it is true that other autonomic parliaments have already started this path long ago and have already begun to include in their law vigilant social services the social concert. So we don't want socialists here to attribute to us any authorship we don't have. Simply, what we are trying to move to this Parliament is a concern that exists in the whole of the third sector, in the social initiative entities, and a solution to that anxiety that comes from the application of the Community directive and the political will that I am sure that among all of us will be able to conjugate here today. Nothing else and thank you very much.
ParlaMint-ES-GA_2015-09-15-DSPG120.u14	Thank you, Mrs Gallego. Round of parliamentary groups. Joint Group, Mrs. Martínez García.
ParlaMint-ES-GA_2015-09-15-DSPG120.u15	Thank you, President. Good morning to all and to all. On June 15th, World Day of Awareness of Abuse and Abuse in Ageing is celebrated. Older people may be more vulnerable to mistreatment depending on their health status, their physical or emotional needs, and the social and family network. 5% of people over 65 suffer from ill-treatment, especially dependent persons, especially women, who account for 2 out of every 3 cases according to the Spanish Society of Family and Community Medicine. At the first consensus conference on the ill-treated elderly person, held in the State in 1995, it was agreed to define abuse of the elderly person as any act or omission that produces harm, whether intentionally or not, practised on persons aged 65 or over, who occur in the family, community or institutional environment, which undermines or endangers the physical, psychic integrity, as well as the principle of autonomy or the rest of the individual's fundamental rights, found objectively or perceived subjectively. The impact of the crisis and austerity policies on the living conditions of older people is a clear example of institutional abuse. It is precisely socio-health matters and pensions where much of the cuts have been focused on. However, care for an increasing elderly population in need of them is placed in the hands of the private initiative. Private companies that act with absolute opacity: they do not report the number of ulcers or how many residents are due to the hospital, nor the reasons why they are due to the hospital; for example, how many cases are due to dehydration. They don't report the number of falls, they don't publish how many residents are subjecting measures and the protocols of their use. A study published by the magazine of the Spanish Society of Geriatrics analyzed the risks and deaths arising from the subjects, and highlighted the following causes: the inability of many older people to know the risks, the lack of supervision, the lack of staff and insufficient training. In Galicia we have already heard several complaints about ill-treatment in care centres for older people, mainly private concerted centres. For example, speaking of the latest, Antonio Godoy de Lugo's day center, with a report of ill-treatment to the old people who came to it. The complaint, filed before the Prosecutor's Office, collects behaviors such as spraying the old people aerosol, throwing them out of their hair, cold water showers and insults. Or the San Rosendo Foundation – created 20 years ago by the Ourense Bishopship in the municipality of the Mosque – denounced for infringing on residents old people, deprived of food and drink, excesses in the supply of sedatives, cold water baths, deliberate omissions of medical care and insults. These situations are often not reported because both the older residents and those who work at these centers are afraid of the consequences. Older people, fear of ill-treatment; workers, fear of dismissal. Because both older people and workers know that the Welsh administration will always be next to the concessionary company; they know that both for the Administration and for the company are the important costs and the accessory to people. Many of these companies offer services that do not provide or force workers – because they are essentially women – to perform functions that do not correspond to them: taxi driver, nurse, hairdresser... and the lack of staff to adequately care for older people in their basic needs is alarming. But, yes, when the time comes for visits, everything is perfect. In addition, Galicia currently has more than 1,400 minors protected in residences or day centres. The vast majority come from destructed families, with progenitors immersed in drug, alcohol, ill-treatment or mental problems. Of the 21,000 children who live today protected by the State Administration, 13,700 are in juvenile centers, mostly in private hands. Entities such as the UN, the European Committee on the Rights of the Child and several NGOs believe that children have become too succulent business for some companies. A report by the Sindic de Cataluña on the protection of children at risk in 2009 had already pointed out that as the form of protection moves away from the family of origin, expenditure increases, but when residential care is approaching, the difference is radical. According to data from 2013, the latest updates, the annual investment in extended families – grandparents, uncles, etc. – is EUR 4,436 per child, while the investment in an alien family is 5,774. Instead, the investment in a child in a foster facility is 47,630 euros, eight times more. The question that arises is, why then, if it's better and cheaper that they're with a family, if they're still institutionalizing minors? Child protection centres are practically in private hands throughout the state. Foundations, associations and businesses account for 70%. For example, of the 58 therapeutic centres for children in the state, 55 are in private hands, most religious. The crisis has increased the number of minors protected. Many parents are afraid to turn to social services for help, especially after such cases as those in Castile-A Mancha, where some parents were taken away from government custody of their children when they came to ask for help because of lack of resources. They're part of the list of disengagement records that doesn't stop growing. The system does not seek to invest in families, but rather invests public money in trying to separate minors from their parents and mothers to deliver minors to private institutions and companies. In the last People's Valor Report, Vulnerable Minors, Deprotection and Criminal Responsibility of 2007, there were serious deficiencies and non-compliances with regulations in juvenile centres in Galicia. Today, eight years later, we have no new assessment of the quality of care in the centres of minors in Galicia, just as there is no study of residences and day centers for older people. The 2014 People's Value Report, on the rights of people with intellectual disabilities, also points to problems for potential users of these services, and among them points out the long waiting lists that occur, especially when the transition from school stage to adulthood is given, with great delays. The Valador considers it essential in this report to increase the squares, especially in the municipalities of greater demand and deficit, and that, in the case of lack of squares, it is offered as an alternative to the same public funding that would correspond to the square and the one that is missing, and not a minor. I will not emphasise that there should not be repeated situations such as that of San Francisco, which has already reported the spokesman of the Socialist Party, but it has been a situation which has ParlaMint-ES-GA_2015-09-15-DSPG120.u16	Thank you, Mrs. Martínez. Parliamentary Group of the Nationalist Block Galego, has the floor to Mrs. Adán Villamarín.
ParlaMint-ES-GA_2015-09-15-DSPG120.u17	Thank you, President. Good morning, ladies and gentlemen. We also want to welcome the people who accompany us and represent the initiative of the third sector, which represents the social initiative. Associations who for years have been performing their work to cover those gaps that have left the state of poor welfare with regard to social services. Today we have a really important debate in this House about what model of public service management we want. We will have it now with regard to social services; subsequently, as already mentioned, with regard to health and the hospital of Vigo. It is true that social services have come late to the welfare state, which has always been precarious, that they have never had such unconditional support from the public sector. In fact, there is this work of families, associations, foundations, to try to cover the gaps left by the public sector, perhaps because social spending has always been regarded as a disaster and not as an investment, not as an opportunity. So, some data have already been mentioned, but in the Spanish state we have the social expenditure per inhabitant of Europe, the lowest percentage of people working in public services for social services, and also the public expenditure with the lowest percentage of GDP for the states around us. That should make us think that social services are not services that are currently endowed with sufficient budget for their maintenance. In fact, today in the debate that we have opened – and we want to contribute to that debate as well – even if there are some considerations about the text of law proposed by the Socialist Party, our vote will be in favour of their taking into consideration, so that we can open a debate during the presentation on how the social concert should be regulated. We believe it is important to say that the law of 2008 made a clear and decisive commitment by public services in the field of social services, but we also understand how the text wants to nude, improve and contribute to defining this social concert. We understand that in this respect are issues that should be more aquilated; that in the social concert, it should be clear that it is not possible that there are entities that are camouflaging companies, but that it is really those entities that come every day providing those non-profit services, without looking as a goal to gain benefit, because – as I said before – in the debate we have about public services we see how these companies seeking benefit – some have already been reported in the field of services to minors, Vigo's hospital is a clear example – there is no benefit to people or a service, but really what there is that spirit of profit on the part of private companies. We therefore believe that it is so important to define the social concert, its complementarity with regard to public services, and - as I said before - to make it very clear in the text itself that, after the process of the Socialist Party's initiative is taken, they are not in the spirit of profit and have no profit activities. I wanted to remember how in foundations like San Rosendo, although they talked about that non-profit, what was behind was a clear search for commercializing social services. From the BNG we have very clear that social services must be public and quality social services, but also – and so I want to make it clear – we recognize this work of social initiative entities, a work that also emerges at a time like late-franquism, when families have the need to articulate accompanying services, seeing the needs that existed, and which were not covered by the State. From this tradition we have come to this moment where, indeed, if it has been done. I was already referring to Mrs Gallego's commemoration of the 25 years of Cogami last week, because we can talk about a work, an experience, a baggage of that deal with people in any field, whether they are people with functional diversity, whether they are older people, whether they are children's attention, families, or any of the social services we're talking about. But I do want to repeat, given the entire debate in this regard in 2008, and the previous work in 2007, in the Social Services Act, that we continue to maintain the importance of the public sector; that the public sector does not exacerbate budgets, quite the contrary, its service, the service it provides, is a high quality service; that the professionals who perform it have a great professionalism; but also – and I want to reiterate this to make it clear – we bear in mind the importance of the social initiative as a complementary area. What we are radically opposed to is the politics of privatization, that public services are in the hands of companies, and referred first to the centres of older people, to the allegations we have of all parliamentary groups – I suppose the People's Party's Parliamentary Group also has them – of different workers or relatives of older people who denounce inappropriate use or treatment by concessional companies, and we can report this to any sector that access social services. That is why we see in this text that the Socialist Party presents an opportunity to debate in the presentation process about what model of social services management we want. We maintain public services, and indeed, in a complementary way and through social concerts, that support for the social initiative. Nothing more and thank you very much.
ParlaMint-ES-GA_2015-09-15-DSPG120.u18	Thank you, Mrs. Adam. Parliamentary Group of Alternative Left Gallega. Mr. Fajardo Recoso has the floor.
ParlaMint-ES-GA_2015-09-15-DSPG120.u19	Good morning to all and to all. First of all, I'm sorry because I have certain doubts that I can finish the intervention, given my physical situation and the first flu esters. I hope you can finish it. We, from the Alternative Gaga de Left, the first thing we want is to recognize to third sector entities what work they do, but we do not want to avoid the fundamental debate that is being brought here today, which is how social services should be organized and how they should be provided. And recognizing the associations in the third sector, those that have no commercial activity, but are simply service providers, because in many cases they have had to self-organise families and people to meet the needs that were not met by the Administration, which we cannot escape is the real debate that is being brought to this House today. That's why this debate, which is well-intentioned, which pursues a noble goal, introduces a drift that can undermine the public concept of social services, which continuously derives from third sector entities the needs that must be met by the Public Administration. We think in an absolutely clear way that what happens is that the public model is being questioned. You're giving a speech to the citizenship where it seems that the only organized and possible response is the one outside the public service conducted by the Administration. These speeches have an obvious danger, which is to reach that state which is unable to meet the social needs of its neighbours and neighbours. And for that reason, recognizing that in the current situation it is essential to recognize the work of third sector entities, and not only fundamental, but it is necessary to differentiate them from those that have a commercial character and that therefore seek the benefit, we must not forget that true solidarity, the only solidarity, is that which is achieved through fair taxes, which allow budgets that pay attention and give capacity to have universal social services that reach all and all. From Alternative Left Galicia what we defend is the change in model, from social services to social rights. We have to introduce the debate that social services have to become social rights, so that any neighbor in this country, who lives in this country, will make it clear that their needs will be met and that they will not, in many cases, have to be self-organised to address issues that should be covered by the State. We have indeed seen the withdrawal of the State from its functions, the breaking of the social pact, through civil mechanisms that conceal fundamental debates to hide them in second matters. We're in an ideological change where it's supposed to be society that addresses these issues, not an administration that's meant for that, and that without that kind of action, it doesn't make sense. We think of an administration that creates social rights, that addresses its neighbours and neighbors, and not an administration that compels society to organize itself to make those issues that don't make the administration itself. In this sense, the initiative presented by the Galician Socialist Party proposes a modification of the Law 13/2008, of the social services of Galicia, in particular the amendment of its Article 30, with the aim of adapting the models of public service provision to the third sector. In that sense, we will support the debate on this initiative, but we will make it clear that this change in order for third sector entities to have a prevalence over others must be for those who do not have a profit. We do not share that social services, social services, are another element of the market, and we can never share it. We do not share that companies that have long spent building now see in social services a business niche, and therefore we are not willing to speculate with social services. That's why we're going to try to improve this proposal during your debate, if it's attended by this House. To improve the proposal to promote access to tenders to those entities that obviously have no kind of profit, but to provide services and attention to citizenship, which is being forgotten by their administration. In that sense, the definition of these entities must be clear. It has to be clear which are understood as non-profit and which is not. And they have to equip themselves with mechanisms that favor these entities in front of others. You can't treat things that aren't the same. It can't be that companies compete on equal terms with non-profit entities, and that's why we're going to try to improve this proposal. What we really understand is that in social services the Public Administration must provide the sufficient needs that this population has, and therefore one thing is what Alternative Left Galeg and another the current situation, and Alternative Left Galeg will seek to ensure that these kinds of services are absolutely public and are carried out by the Administration through mechanisms that allow co-existence with civil society. But in the current situation, where this reality is impossible for the People's Party and other previous governments, who have been destroying public services and outsourcing virtually everything that has to do with social action, we are in the need to provide sufficient mechanisms for third sector entities so that in this way people can be cared for and not in situations that can put their quality of life and even their own life at risk. We therefore want to denounce the lack of public administration, which leaves these entities the initiative that should be of the administration itself, and therefore once again society demonstrates that it is above and before the administration itself and shows that it is able to organize and establish models that should be copied by the Administration to give sufficient attention to those people who need it. The administration must provide sufficient services, which it does not do today. And what you can't imagine is that social services are treated as mere figures, as numbers, and not as people who need them and who are therefore their beneficiaries. This situation is worse now, when the private and commercial initiative sees in the provision of social services a business, a business that comes to the attention of people with disabilities, older people, but also people affected by inequality. Because we don't want to talk only about poverty, we want to talk about inequality; because inequality is the one that causes poverty, because inequality has to do with that way that some people are made with greater purchasing capacity on the basis of removing others, and therefore these commercial entities who pursue a profit spirit also see in poverty a business niche. In this situation we want to intervene. In that situation, in that law of social services in Galicia, we must make it clear that there must be barriers and that speculation cannot be allowed with the needs of others, of the social majority in this country. In this sense, clear examples were set here of how the Administration of the Union prioritized companies that only wanted to be business against self-organized families to care for their dependents. Questions that have happened in Vigo, in Ourense, even in Vilagarcia de Arousa, made it clear that the Administration's only interest is to be able to round the figures of its budget even though behind that round of figures is the outsourcing of services and the placement of people with fundamental needs in the hands of companies that only they pursue is the business. And one company, when you have to put a user's needs ahead of your business, we're clear that in many cases you put the business above your needs. In this country they're turning dependents into commodities. In this country they are planted as one more element of negotiating the dependents or users of social services, and these are the issues that we must introduce in this reform. We are faced with the real burial of the public concept of social services, and that is why we will support this initiative for discussion, but in its debate we will pursue the introduction of mechanisms that guarantee two issues: firstly, the absolutely altruistic character of these organizations, which will be those that are and have priority attention; and secondly, the barriers sufficient to avoid mercantilism, to avoid speculation with social services and to avoid – as I have said earlier – that social service users are understood as pure commodities. We oppose speculation about social services. Nothing else and thank you very much.

ParlaMint-ES-GA_2015-09-15-DSPG120.u21	Thank you, Madam President. Counsellor, good morning. Good morning, ladies and gentlemen, people who accompany us in the rostrum and representatives of social entities. First of all, I would like to make it clear that the President of the Union is currently meeting with the Director General of FAO signing a collaborative agreement to promote initiatives relating to agriculture, fisheries and food to develop these areas in the least developed countries. So we know that at present the Labour and Welfare Councillor, who is responsible for the Government of the Union in this matter that we are debating, is present, and we know that the President of the Union is signing a very important agreement for many people in many countries who need it most. So you have to understand that there are issues that cannot be criticized in this Parliament. To begin with, I would like to remind you at this debate, about a social services law which was adopted in 2008, some issues; above all, to remind those parliamentary groups that supported the Government at that time, in 2008: that the Social Services Act establishes – as set out in its Article 29 – that social services will be provided by public administrations directly or indirectly through various types of procurement of public service management established in the regulation of public sector contracts, in particular through the mode of concert. And we continue, in Article 31 states that, for reasons of duly justified public interest, it is possible to subscribe to individual agreements with social-initiative entities for the provision of social services where it is not possible or appropriate to promote public competition. Article 33 states that the prices of tenders may indicate the preference for the award of tenders submitted by non-profit entities, but whenever and when they are equal to the conditions which are economically most advantageous. We're talking about bidding plugs, criteria in case of dismissal. And Article 32 of that law adopted in 2008 establishes the ability of commercially-initiated entities to act as social service providers. Ladies and gentlemen, this law makes no distinction or prevalence between the social initiative entities and the commercial initiative entities. So, you, who speak in your initiative that this law must be amended, a law that you yourself have drafted and passed, a law that in 2008 has not established any kind of prevalence to social entities, now in your initiative they ensure that the legislator, when he drafted this law, tried to ensure that, in the absence of a sufficient public offer, this law could be completed with the private initiative before the commercial initiative. So we wonder what the model is in this case of the Socialist Parliamentary Group, because in its drafting and in the adoption of this Social Services Act you have not established any kind of prevalence for social entities, however, now in your proposed amendment of the law, they ensure that the intention was to ensure the primordiality of social entities to the detriment of the commercial initiative. What we just saw is that this is a left-wing law, approved by a left-wing government – socialist and nationalist – that applied and is applying a People's Party government. And now these own parliamentary groups that have drawn up, drafted and approved this law want to amend it, but what we just checked in this debate is that they do not even agree on how it should be amended. Because two completely different formulas have been heard here: on the one hand, the formula that social services have to be 100% public – hence closing the door to indirect management and commercial initiative – and on the other hand, we have heard that commercial initiative and indirect management must be maintained, but giving priority to social entities. So you will understand that the first thing you have to do, if you propose an amendment of such an important law as Galician Social Services Act, is to agree, because we believe that changing this law is something of a size enough to achieve as broad and as broad an understanding as possible. With regard to the proposal to amend the Socialists, of course, we agree with the People's Parliamentary Group that social entities should be prioritized in the provision of services in Galicia, but we also believe that this must be done with the maximum legal guarantees, and of course by studying the text; we have many doubts about the legal security of this proposal. We consider and share the need for a new scheme for social institutions; we believe that it is necessary to recognize the fundamental role of social initiative entities in the provision of social services in Galicia; and we understand – and so we will – by drafting a new proposal – that a third way can be opened by putting in place a system of collaboration with entities in the third sector. But the amendment proposed by the Socialist Group includes a formula, that of the social concert, of which we have doubts about its survival in the current legal framework. We have doubts as to whether the social concert can be a public sector contract form, because the Public Sector Contracts Act, in its Article 2, states that public sector contracts are costly, whatever their legal nature. Can you therefore include as an administrative contract a non-profit contract, since the contract law itself collects that they are all of an expensive nature? The European Union directive, of which you speak, on public contracts also lays down in Article 2 which are costly contracts concluded in writing between one or more economic operators and one or more taxing powers whose objective is the execution of works, supplies of production or services. The Socialist proposal eliminates the competition system as part of existing legislation and incorporates the social concert as a type of contract in the public sector. In addition, you speak of principles that would be met to establish social concerts that are already established by the Government of Galicia. They speak of a social concert scheme, which is necessary for authorization, as well as accreditation by the entity, the provision of means and resources of the ownership of the centres, which is now necessary. They also talk about considering in the general legislation on subsidies and administrative procedures the regulation of conventions, which is already being regulated. It is to reiterate the current rules. And in addition, as in the previous point, this point aims to regulate agreements and subsidies already provided for in the current legislation. So, in relation to the Socialist Group's proposal, we are going to vote against it, because from our point of view it generates quite a few legal doubts and because it is the amendment of a law, such as the Social Services Act, with its importance and scope, or to open a process of dialogue with the sector to achieve the broadest possible consensus, in seeking to prioritize non-profit social initiative entities. Of course, our group commits itself to drafting a new proposal with the maximum legal guarantees, after sitting with the third sector entities; a specific partnership framework with social initiative entities for the provision of social services as an alternative system to the public service management contract; a consensus framework with the entities and within the underlying legality, with all guarantees, because we know that the autonomous communities you are talking about, studying their changes, which are not the same as those currently presenting the Socialist Group, have presented repairs by the Consultative, which has caused some legal problems or some legal gaps. We therefore believe that the Social Services Act should be amended, that third sector entities should be prioritized in the provision of social services, but we want to do so with the maximum legal guarantees, and of course we cannot approve your proposal because it causes us many doubts in that sense. And, of course, when it comes to the provision of social services in our community, it should be recalled that 94% of the disability squares in Galicia are managed by social entities who have presented themselves to open public competitions, and 100% of the juvenile squares...
ParlaMint-ES-GA_2015-09-15-DSPG120.u22	Go finish, please.
ParlaMint-ES-GA_2015-09-15-DSPG120.u23	...are also managed by social initiative entities. We therefore share that social initiative entities must have a greater role in the management of social services, but we also believe that changing such an important law as the Social Services Act requires consensus with third sector entities and, of course, legal guarantees. Nothing else and thank you very much.

The opinion of Mrs. Councillor for the Environment and the Sea, on his own request, to report on the current situation in the sector and on the assessment of the latest measures proposed at European and State level for the milk sector
ParlaMint-ES-GA_2015-09-15-DSPG120.u60	You have the floor for your first intervention by the advisor of the Middle Rural and Sea, Mrs. Rosa Quintana Carballo.
ParlaMint-ES-GA_2015-09-15-DSPG120.u61	Thank you, Madam President. Ladies and gentlemen, very good morning to all of you. I compare today again in this House to account for a situation that affects around 10,000 livestock farms in this community, which has focused on the concerns of some 60,000 people linked to the Greek agribusiness. I was saying a month ago, and I repeat now, that there is a problem in the Greek milk sector. We have a conjunctural and structural problem, and we have to deal with them. It must therefore be made clear today and before this Parliament that, in conjunctural terms, no one denies that we go through a moment of adjustment and adaptation between supply and demand, and consequently the price, which makes the situation difficult, complicated and negative. In addition, structurally, we have a problem of organisational vertebration of a highly professional producer sector with cutting-edge farms and an industry that is excessively based on the packaging of liquid milk. In the face of these two situations, we have to act and we are acting. We're acting in a determined and planned way that wants to leave behind how many sirens as those who seem to offer those who give simple and direct solutions to complex and polyedral problems at their source. We've been, we're, and we're going to be working so that the yellow sheep and sheep can go ahead and live decently. We only work with that interest and with that premise, we are on their side and we are dedicated to the measures put in place, with the dedication of many people, technicians and management team of this council. I was talking about this work in my previous appearance, and I would put in nearly 650 million euros the total amount that the Union has already, or will come, to the Greek dairy sector in the next few years. These include the approaches provided for in the Rural Development Programme, which account for more than EUR 500 million by 2020. Thus, between the already paid and what is planned to be paid in the short term amount to almost EUR 150 million, of which 95.5 were direct aid to the exploitation; and, moreover, EUR 70 million that we will pay before the end of the year, which will increase the amount to EUR 17,200 per holding average. But we didn't start working yesterday, as some say. Since 2009, Galicia has taken measures to improve the competitiveness of farms, its financial and liquidity capacity and the commercial structure of the sector. It has advanced in the improvement of the territorial base of farms through land mobility laws and the Law of improvement of the land structure, and the inclusion of land of unknown owners. Also, in order to improve the financial and liquidity capacity of the sector, the decree regulating the procedure for the recognition and registration of producer organisations has been published, and the brand "gaga 100%" has been launched, which supports the identification of the differentiated quality milk produced in Galicia. Furthermore, the Council pointed out that since 2012 the Ministry of Agriculture, Food and the Environment has approved three Royal Decrees to supplement the regulation of the Law on measures to improve the functioning of the food chain. We put on the table a short, medium and long-term planning of measures, which conjugate more than twenty measures or blocks of measures in which the Union and the Central Government are working to meet the objective of getting this sector forward, and complementing other ways that must lead us to a complete change in the sector itself to avoid cyclically repeating this situation. In connection with a problem that largely covers the German and Spanish borders, the Ministry has reinforced a road to work in which it was already located and has led it to seek allies in Europe and to demand support measures from the European Commission. But these proposed administrative measures must be accompanied, if we want to avoid falling into the same well within a few months, by this complete reformulation of the milk price line and the contractual and negotiating relations between the parties in the milk sector. So by moving forward in the work that has been done for months, in these last few weeks, the Mapra and Junta are working together with the producer, industrial and distributor sector to find an output that articulates a new model of creating the most stable and equitable value of milk in the distribution of that value. On the line of work in Europe, Minister García Tejerina met in Madrid on 28 August with her counterparts from France, Italy and Portugal to join positions towards the Council of Ministers of Extraordinary Agriculture, which took place on 7 September. This meeting bore fruit that the four countries, which increased to 15 thanks to the ministry's work among European partners, had consolidated the defence of the same position that Galicia shares and whose fundamental requests were based in an important way on the review of intervention prices for a limited period of time of skimmed milk powder. In addition, private storage for cheese was also claimed to be reopened, with the aim of withdrawing supply from the market, and it was intended to inject liquidity into holdings with an increase of 50% in advance of the CAP aid. In addition, there was a call for an improvement in the promotion of milk products and the identification of origin, in addition to consolidating the operation of the Milky Market Observatory to obtain better data and provide better information. Finally, the ministers assembled at the meeting called for the establishment of a high-level group to monitor and analyse the situation of the milk market in this post-harvest scenario. In this sense, the next stop by the minister on this line of work in Europe was the meeting with Agriculture Commissioner Phil Hogan, which took place on 31 August. It is true that we have not had good news at this meeting, since the Commissioner has shown himself unwilling to increase the intervention price, but he is willing to improve the system of advance payments from the CAP. In addition, the Commissioner himself called for Spain's referral from the Food Chain Act to see the possibility of transposing it, mutatis mutandis, to the Community aid, as it had done with the advance payment of the CAP itself made by Galicia in 2009. This work has ended with the conclusion of the Council of Ministers of Agriculture of the European Union on 7 September. In this council, an important set of measures was agreed, with an allocation of EUR 500 million, which can be classified into three types: measures to solve the lack of liquidity of the owners, measures to stabilize markets and measures to eliminate supply chain problems. Galician holdings will benefit directly or indirectly from all these measures, among which there can be particular emphasis on new direct aid for milk beef farmers, as well as the European Commission's study of flexibility measures to make payments for CAP aid. But in addition to these measures which the European Union is approaching and in the face of the difficulty of raising the intervention price, the Council approved improvements in storage for skimmed milk powder, with an effect equivalent to what would have meant by that price rise, as they increase aid and ensure an appropriate storage time. In the same way, and achieved the other objectives put forward, the Commissioner, by the meeting countries in Madrid, was also confronted with the importance of opening up new markets in third countries, as well as increasing efforts to eliminate non-tariff barriers in those countries. Finally, the start-up of a high-level group will be focused on clear and specific issues, including funding for recipients or financial and risk management instruments, in addition to addressing also supply chain problems to propose strategic actions aimed at increasing their transparency and redistributing benefits. It must be said that in the coming days these measures will be taking place at various meetings of the Community institutions, both the Commission and the informal Council of Agriculture, which is being held today. It is therefore necessary to assess positively the decisions of the European Union, which came to refer to the work done by Spain in this way and pointed out that no country of the European Union has put on the table as many measures as Spain has done. But the line of work in Europe, if important, was the main one developed by the Ministry and the Union. The main efforts were focused on the state, and there came out two fundamental fruits: a line of direct aid and a proposal for agreement that can lead to the balance of the construction of prices to the sector and consequently to sustainability. For that, a first meeting was held between the Ministry, the producer, industrial and distributor sector, which took place on August 18th. At that meeting, a proposal was made to grant direct aid of about 300 euros per cow to those farms that were on a low price margin, a proposal that was improved by the sector's arrivals. So at that meeting we started a process of improving this aid line, which resulted in an incorporation to them with a smaller content of those farms that have problems of profitability due to economic commitments derived from improvement plans and other depreciations. This short-term line of work thus added this line of work complements and helps, in part, to sustain the sector at these difficult times by injecting liquidity, knowing that the real key to the future will be given by the agreement between the parties to make price building sustainable. For this reason, at the first meeting, already in August, the participants were given the draft proposal for a sectoral agreement on the stability and sustainability of the milk value chain and milk products, including the proposal for commitments that should be made by representatives of the dairy food distribution companies and also by cooperatives and agricultural organisations to re-evaluate the price of milk and milk products. In this sense, the proposal for an agreement raises important issues that we must consider, as the sector demanded them and they have already been picked up from the outset. The agreement promotes the identification of the source of milk in a clear and visible way and for more information to the consumer, it transparents the value chain with the near transfer prices of the industry to the distribution, so we'll know how much industry sells and the consumer will see where the benefit remains. Finally, this agreement would be almost immediate, since it comes into force at ten days, so if the signature is not delayed, it will enter into force before 1 October. In addition, the document was also drawn to the agreement which obtained some important terms of progress with regard to August. In this sense, it has been pointed out that the distribution will be integrated into a working group with Inlac, which can be a big step towards their integration into this entity, which would be very positive. Similarly, the industry undertakes to transfer and share with the creditors the re-valorization of the products obtained from the distribution and the integration of the first buyers into the document, which would be part of that transparency and progress in contractualization. In addition, this new draft was accompanied by an important issue, which consists of a firm commitment from the Map to be set up as a guarantor of the agreement, and it has to show the decision to raise to the real decree the basic issues that the agreement comes up with, which, by giving it normative rank, would create the obligation to comply with and the possibility to sanction. Since then, and in the light of the opinion of the meeting participants, we can be close to a positive agreement for all, which allows us to see how the price is built and to bring transparency to the consumer by better defining their ability to choose. We are faced with a proposal for an agreement that, similar to French, could give rise to a historical understanding, as was already outstanding the agreement reached at the beginning of last week in Inlac between producers and industry, and whose terms were collected in full in the draft presented last Thursday. With this support and taking into account the public transmission and integration of small flakes into the document, it seems that the text has the agreement of a part of the producer sector, of the whole industry and of distribution, and of a key figure as cooperatives. Both lines of work were analyzed and discussed in Galicia with the producer sector on two occasions: last Wednesday and Friday, while the situation and the measures proposed by the Galician Union with distribution, industry and the producer sector were analysed on 5 August. This ongoing dialogue follows the lines of around 100 meetings with dairy agents and representatives if they have dealt with matters of interest to the sector itself in the coming year. Furthermore, Galicia's proposals and demands have also been brought to the ministry in relation to the European line of work and the agreement. We understand that it has yielded positive fruit for the livestock and will bring more stability to the sector. On the other hand, we continue to work so that the projected measures will go forward as soon as possible and can bear fruit for the gainers in this difficult situation they are going through. Thus, the Board of Trustees has already given green light to the corresponding budgetary commitment to enable aid to inject liquidity into dairy farms with approaches to loan moratoriums linked to the improvement of farms and the incorporation of young people into agricultural activity. These arrivals may benefit holders of dairy holdings who request a period of absence of up to two years in the amortisation of the loan granted on their day to support investments in improvement of holdings and incorporation of young people into agriculture. In total, the beneficiaries may be about 1,100 milk holding owners, who will not have to pay for two years, neither amortization nor interest. In addition, we have already launched the campaign to promote Greek milk, where your lordships will have heard or seen announcements of promotions of "Galga 100%" and which will soon be completed with differentiation of brands that can be used in the supermarket lines. In the same way we are also moving forward to have this month's promotional initiative of small tetrabriks of "Galga 100%" to promote the consumption of milk among children and which will be added to one carried out by Inlac itself. Therefore, Fogga's staff are redoubled efforts to enable the advance payment of the CAP on October 16, as we also work on the direct injection line of liquidity, which together with the help of the Ministry per cow, will reduce the level of the economic suffocation that drowns certain dairy farms. But we know that with this work the problem is not solved forever. The stability and sustainability of the sector must come to an agreement to improve the value chain and its construction and to make structural changes to the organizational capacity of the producer sector and the formula for transforming milk. All this, which is collected in the Ministry's proposal for an agreement, leads us to collect the widest possible group we've ever had, taking advantage of everything that legality allows us in favour of the dairy sector and even overburdening similar agreements from countries around us. We are referring specifically to two very specific problems. One, which has to do with pensions paid in Germany by returned migrants before 1 January 2013, in which a new Spanish-German bilateral treaty entered into force. Pursuant to Article 19 of the previous treaty applicable to that date, it was clearly and explicitly established that these pensions were not subject to taxation in the Spanish State and therefore should not be included in the income statements of the IRPF by the persons receiving them. In spite of that, the Tax Agency did a different, contradictory interpretation, as I said earlier, in numerous judgments of higher courts of justice. But here we find ourselves with a perversion of how the People's Party uses the rule of law not to govern legality, but to use the rule of law according to its political interests. The counsellor of Fairy himself in that appeal recognized that the State had never resorted to such judgments in cassation, because, since they are not creators of judicial doctrine the judgments of the higher courts of justice, it would not be obliged to apply the content of that judgment itself. So I strongly affirm, on behalf of my group, that there is a perversion on the part of the State and the Tax Agency of the so-called rule of law to harass, in this case, tremendously vulnerable people, to force them individually to have to file claims in the litigation-administrative courts so that their rights can be compensated and stop the violation of those rights that occurred when they were forced to tax pensions received before 1 January 2013. Something similar occurs in relation to persons receiving an invalidity pension from foreign countries, that the lack of recognition through the expertise of the General Social Security Treasury to match them, to see the degree of equalization that they would have in the Spanish State, is forcing many persons with disabilities, disabilities recognized in the foreign that would be equivalent to absolute disability and great disability in the Spanish State, and that, therefore, at the expense of Article 7 of Law 35/2006 of the income tax of natural persons should be exempt from taxation, we are seeing how they systematically require them to include these pensions for invalidity in their income statement. So we're also going through this Vigilant Article of the Income Tax Law of natural persons without the Tax Agency getting any kind of reason either, and accepting also in this area judicial resolutions that are being produced in the sense that they have to be returned that money, unduly paid, to the people who have done so. We, of course, consider that, given the characteristics of returned emigrants, because they are elderly people, who lived much of their life in other states with the functioning of the administration in light years of the operation of the administration of the Spanish State, we believe that by the Tax Agency, instead of torpeating, as is being done by that agency, the exercise of rights and compensation of the rights of these persons, which should be joint instruments so that they can be exercised more easily; and of course, so that neither pensioners with pensions of Germany nor pensioners with pensions of disability, whatever the state of origin, to exercise their rights, to have individual, to have, on a case-by-case basis, the person, and still incuring in excess of those who have already made them incur the Tax Agency, resort to justice to comply with the applicable legality.
