ParlaMint-ES-GA_2015-09-15-DSPG120.u146	You have the floor, for your first intervention, the health adviser, Mrs. Rocío Mosquera Álvarez.
ParlaMint-ES-GA_2015-09-15-DSPG120.u147	President, ladies and gentlemen, good afternoon. Good afternoon, everyone. I'm here today to account for the transfer and launch process of the Álvaro Cunqueiro Hospital. My initial intention was to come and explain it once this process is finished, but in the face of the confusion generated, and as there is nothing, absolutely nothing, to hide, I come today to inform this House. As a first resort, there's a fact that I don't obviously want to shed any light on. The new hospital is part of the University Hospital Complex of Vigo, it is public, it is owned by Sergas, and therefore it is owned by all the vigues, the inhabitants of its metropolitan health area and all the Welsh and Welsh. Vigo now owns a hospital that many Spanish cities want to have. The public health professionals have at their disposal the infrastructure with greater capacity and potential, within their genre, as well as the progress and possibility of the future that has taken place in Spain in recent years, and that has been faced with the difficulty added to it in full economic recession. I live with an infrastructure that will provide healthcare – and in fact it is already lending – to the more than 600,000 neighbors of that city and its area of influence. Everyone had been very clear for many years that Vigo's area needed a new hospital and that one couldn't wait any longer; a need not to be answered even in the years of greater economic kindness. Let's make some history. The hospital project is framed within the Sectoral Project of Supreme Incidence, the PSIS, approved in February 2009. In parallel with this PSIS, the environmental impact declaration was submitted. In addition to these two documents, when we arrived in 2009 at the Galego Health Service, there was a basic project for the new Vigo hospital, approved in October 2008, but that it had not been submitted to public information. We had to do it on January 28, 2010. As a matter of fact, there was a framework agreement between the Galician Council, through the health councils, the Galego Health Service and the Territorial Policy Council, Public Works and Transport, and the Vigo Council for the construction of the new hospital and the staffing of its infrastructures signed in March 2006, which defined the works that each had to do. In this agreement, it was specified that the Health Council assumed the drafting of the sectoral project of supra-municipal incidence and expropriated land, setting out in its sixth clause that the management of all services in the complex will correspond to Sergas, for which it will use legally established management formulas. CPTOPT assumed access and the Vigo Council assumed the necessary supplies, the necessary services for the construction and operation of the hospital, in particular water supply and sanitation, as well as the provision and supply of city gas services, electricity, optical fibre channeling and any other necessary or essential for the construction and operation of the hospital. In addition, in its eighth clause, it states that the Vigo Council will give up free of charge, free of charge and taxes, those lands within the scope of the sectoral project that it has at the time of signing this convention, as well as those others which the Council opts to acquire for any title. Because of this agreement, we had to deal with the process of expropriation of land, which we ended on October 5, 2010, with an amount of 7.5 million euros. It's the first time that a council doesn't provide counseling the ground for the construction of a health infrastructure, whether it's a health center or a hospital. It is one more example of the absolute lack of support that we have had from the very first moment on by that corporation, which for all these years has refused to take on its commitments, neither those collected in the agreement nor any other. Also in the Galego Health Service there was a study of economic-financial viability to build the hospital by the PFI procedure, a study that curiously the bipartite commissioned the company PricewaterhouseCoopers and which so much demonized some later. It is true that there was also another feasibility study to be carried out by the SPI, but without a single euro for that. In these diatrics, time has passed, and they haven't been able to build it either by one system or by another, and in moments of economic kindness. There were also several functional plans, developed between 2006 and 2009 and carried out by Elola Consultants and Intersalus. It must be remembered that, in the face of the economic crisis, much of the projects that were underway throughout Spain had to be reformulated, and many times even had to be abandoned. It wasn't Vigo's case. From the Government of Galician Union, we have considered from the very first moment that this was one of our priorities, the need to provide Vigo with a new hospital that would enable us to overcome the existing deficiencies, and we started with the process. The first analysis document is already in September 2009. Several were the reasons that led us to choose the model of public-private collaboration, the so-called PFI. The most relevant were: having a new hospital in the worst known economic scenario, with the conviction that with the traditional formula it would not be possible; that is, the decision was to make or not to do the hospital. And the second reason is to be able to do it as soon as possible, and of course, in much less time than by the usual public procurement system, than we had a very recent experience in Lugo, where it took more than ten years. Thus, in January 2010 we put the public exhibition on the study of economic and financial viability of the award of public works for the drafting of the project, financing, construction and exploitation of certain non-clinical services complementary to the new hospital in Vigo. And in May 2010 we published in the DOG, the PDO and the BOE the bidding of the concession of public works and the drafting of the technical project, financing, construction and exploitation of certain non-clinical services of the University Hospital Complex of Vigo. I would like to point out that in these documents it is collected – as it cannot be otherwise – the field of recruitment, which are the non-clinical services object of management; I remind you that they are the restoration, laundry and lingerie, the cleaning, the maintenance of electromedicine, the general maintenance, the destabilization, destabilization and disinfection, the internal and external transport and the auxiliary management, security, the supply and energy management, the water supply and treatment plant, the maintenance of printing and reprographic equipment and the conservation of avenues and gardens. And I insist again that, of these twelve services, nine were already being lent by external companies. These documents also collect the maximum annual fee payable to the dealership. A maximum annual fee of EUR 72.87 million was set in the tender, of which EUR 46.44 was to be built and 26.43 was to provide non-sanitary services, with EUR 1.20 million in deduction of costs for commercial holdings; i.e. the total to be paid was set at EUR 71.66 million. In the adjudication, the AMC reduced to €60.4 million: €36.2 of the work and 24.2 of the non-health services, with a deduction for commercial holdings of €3.9 million; i.e. to pay €56.5 million. This, of course, without IVE, because this one was changing. On these commercial holdings is included the parking lot, clearly showing that the tariff had to be the average rate of the city of Vigo. All these documents, the study of economic-financial viability and recruitment fees, were on public display in the profile of Sergas' contractor and were published as established by the legislation in force. So, I insist, this is known to everyone who's wanted to see you since 2010. The contract with the adjudicatory UTE was signed on 21 February 2011. The process involved a lot of effort, work and illusion from many people, from many professionals, who had a common goal: to equip Vigo and southern Galicia with a pointing hospital and with the best supplies. It was carried out with all guarantees and maximum transparency, complying – as could not be otherwise – with the recast text of the Public Sector Contracts Act. It has all the favourable preceptive reports: from staff technicians, legal counsel, intervention, and for greater abundance in these types of contracts, also with the favourable report of the General Intervention of the State, which was issued in May 2010. Indeed, ladies and gentlemen, in May 2010, when Mr. Rodríguez Zapatero still ruled, obviously. I don't think you can take any further advantage of the legal security of this contract. But let's continue with the project. The re-enactment was signed on 24 June 2011, and the work began effectively on 24 September 2011. Once the contract has been signed, the TEU negotiates the financing, and in the worst financial scenario that this country has experienced, it was able to close it on August 1, 2013. The date of completion of the work was last April 24, 2015. I would like to point out that it has been built, despite the difficulties of the moment and the absolute lack of collaboration, especially of the Vigo Council, in a record time: less than four years from the beginning of the works to the delivery of them to Sergas, half the time limit than other similar and recent works. The last comparable hospital put into operation was the Central University Hospital of Asturias, and it took him to build almost twelve years, three times more than the Cunqueiro Alvaro. On April 25, the deadline for the start-up of the infrastructure of this new public hospital and the transfer began. Thus, 70 days later, June 29th was the first day of hospital care work. The first 65 patients received attention in the services of Anesthesia, Nephrology and in the unit of Hemodialysis, while activity began at the hospital of the Pathological Anatomy Service. This date, historic for the health of Vigo and for the Galician, has been developed with total normality. The transfer process of a hospital is a huge technical and logistical complexity. We're talking about the progressive transfer of hundreds of patients who need hospitalization and also of the hundreds of professionals responsible for their attention, in addition to the necessary equipment and furniture. That's why it was considered that summer months were the most suitable to carry it out, since the number of patients entered and the activity of the center is smaller than in other times of the year. In Vigo this complexity is increased by the very characteristics of assistance. It's not about changing from building to building, but it's necessary to reorganize and unify in a single centre the activity being carried out in two, three, and even in some cases four centers. There's a need to homogenize work procedures that are often very sharp and integrate teams of people who have never worked together. This undoubtedly increases uncertainty and resistance. But we also made it clear – and so the responsible ones conveyed it to us – that there was no other way to do it. This reorganization is part of the differentiation of buildings according to the pathology to be met, instead of the city hall of origin. It is established that at the Hospital Álvaro Cunqueiro, the Emergency Service will be unified, so far divided, with two entry doors; the Intensive Medicine units, also divided; the most complex surgical activity and that pathology in general more complicated or with greater technical requirements. At the Hospital do Mexeiro, the short-term and outpatient surgery and medical hospitalization of chronic, psychopathological and advanced-age patients, as well as those specialties with a higher outpatient burden, are concentrated. And also radiotherapy, because of the technical difficulties of moving the equipment. Hospital Nicolás Peña will perform psychiatric hospitalization, rehabilitation and residential care. This same principle responds to laboratory planning, designing a single laboratory in the University Hospital Complex of Vigo, with two perfectly differentiated parts: a quick response for all those tests that have to have results in less than six hours, and the most complex, that require individualized equipment and that will be located in the Cunqueiro Álvaro. And a great Core lab, which responds to all the scheduled analytical tests – we remember that 80% come from primary care – that will be located in the Mexoneiro and that, as has just been demonstrated in many other centres in Galicia and outside Galicia, it improves the traceability and efficiency of laboratories. Therefore, the entire process of transfer and reorganization of care has been programmed with precision, rigor and in a very detailed way. And this proclamation was presented and agreed with the professionals during the months of May and June and was presented to the press by the manager on June 5, 2015. But it is quite true that from the very first moment we had clear that the calendar was subject to the fulfilment of four guiding principles: safety for patients and professionals, increasing complexity, minimization of transfer periods and the involvement of professionals. Of them, obviously, the main thing was to guarantee the safety conditions of patients and professionals. From June 29 to August 28 – two months – the process was carried out strictly within the programme, so the activity of external consultations, diagnostic tests, day-to-day hospitals and all conventional hospitalizations are already in the new hospital and in operation. That is, it took about 75% of the transfer without larger incidences and according to the established calendar. On 28 August, the University Hospital Complex of Vigo proposed to reorder the last phase of the planned transfer for the next week, according to the recommendations of the preventive medicine service and according to the professionals of the centre, in order to ensure the guiding principle of this whole process: the safety of patients. This very morning, contacts were made with the various involved in this measure: the preventive medicine service, the services and units that were to be transferred to the next week and the services and units that were already performing their activity in the Cunqueiro Álvaro, and so on, at the last hour of the morning, proceed to inform the commission of the centre. Therefore, we understand that the criticism of lack of information or transparency is not at all groundless. Transparency, ladies and gentlemen. The measure was taken by the agency after environmental controls carried out by the preventive medicine service, not counting 100% of the negative results of critical units, and can be sure that only when this information is fully available will a new transfer date be established. The hospital's most critical units remain pending: the surgical block, the intensive care and resuscitation and emergency units. The emergencies because they are subject to the previous ones, and the surgical block and critical units because they require security controls that are necessary to carry out very adjusted times, and if they are not totally negative, they invalidate their put into operation. In fact, these controls in the units already operating are done periodically, and, if they are the positive results, they are closed. I repeat, therefore, that the priority is the safety of the patient and that it is considered appropriate to carry out the transfer of these units together, according to the schedule presented, and not individually, the transfer program was postponed until all controls considered necessary. This, and no other, is the decision that must be taken by a responsible administration and that must be expected from the Galician Health Service. This advisor, therefore, feels secure from the decisions made, ensures the safety of patients who will be transferred to the Álvaro Cunqueiro Hospital and also those who are already in the hospital and fully disengage those who intend to desist from the Greek public health. So I want to make it very clear that, once all controls are correct, with the visa and pleasure of professionals – especially in this case of preventive medicine – the process is resumed. And that's why, according to the manager of the University Hospital Complex of Vigo, together with the head of preventive medicine service, next Monday, September 21, the transfer will resume following the schedule. In the last few days, we've seen the relocation of the different units of the Mexoeiro, even yesterday's dermatology has moved. It is imperative to point out, for the tranquility of citizenship, that, as well as underlining the manager of the University Hospital Complex of Vigo and the heads of services repeatedly these days, the needs of care, technology and human resources of patients hospitalized in the Cunqueiro Alvaro are fully guaranteed with the resources that are now available. Another of the issues that has been raised these days was staffing. As far as this is concerned, I have to say that the bidding of medical equipment and clinical furniture has been carried out in different phases grouped into batches according to the technology in question. All this equipment plan is estimated to achieve an investment of more than 45 million euros, and competitions have been held in recent months, always complying with current regulations. I have to say, moreover, that all the appeals brought against these tenders have been failed in favour of Sergas, except for the laboratory, where we have decided to accept the Central Administrative Court of Contractual Resources resolution and bring it out again. In addition, the equipment plan is completed, as it cannot be otherwise, with the equipment transfer plan of the other centres; all that is in operation conditions and that has been agreed with the professionals, as well as the technological update of them. Finally, with this new public health centre, as we always insist, we respond with vision of the future to a historical need and justice to Galicia's most populated health area. I think the figures are very powerful about the magnitude and capacity of this backend. The total built surface is 280,000 square meters, three times more than the General Hospital. It has as main equipment: 22 surgeries for incoming surgery, 2 integrated and 2 emergency surgeries; 12 delivery units; 2 cesarean surgeries and 5 special rooms; 62 image diagnostic rooms; 150 multichannel sites and a large area of consultations; an area of emergencies that duplicates the current ones; the area of teaching and research and other areas such as parking, kindergarten, heliport, installation center, library, etc.; and a maximum hospitalization capacity of 1,465 beds. We have here, at last, a hospital of the future, with a flexible architectural design, with space reserves to accommodate the growth of services and with the ability to adapt to future needs, but we are also living an unprecedented stage in the health system, in which some seem not to stand in bars to desist the new hospital, and with it the public health system, and with it all the professionals who are working day by day to make the move in the best possible conditions and with the best guarantees of quality and safety. [[Speak words that are not perceived.]] They seem unable to see the potential of having this infrastructure available for health care in Vigo over the next few years, and working to achieve it. At this point, I must state that in the process of transfer they do not seem responsible for actions such as organizing demonstrations, asking for research committees to be set up, asking Europe for facts known four years ago...

[[Speak words that are not perceived.]] G.P. motion. of the Socialists of Galicia, at the initiative of José Luis Méndez Romeu, on the adoption by the Government of Greece of certain measures in the field of local administration
ParlaMint-ES-GA_2015-09-15-DSPG120.u267	This motion was tabled an amendment by the People's Parliamentary Group of Galicia.
ParlaMint-ES-GA_2015-09-15-DSPG120.u268	You have the word, to formulate the motion, Mr. Méndez Romeo.
ParlaMint-ES-GA_2015-09-15-DSPG120.u269	Madam President, ladies and gentlemen, you can be sure that I will not use the name of anyone to turn you into an abusive appeal, either in the context of this debate or into any other. But this group will always complain about any of the members of this House, at least the respect this spokesman tries to have for each of them. Ladies and gentlemen, today, 318 local corporations are normally formed in Galicia. That means there's a plural representation of 3,766 concellers and concellers, and another hundred more in provincial deputies; all of them with a desire to improve the living conditions of their neighbours and neighbours, logically taking into account their own competences and, inescapably, their inappropriate calls. Because the truth is, after all the negative things that the Government of the State has said, and also the Government of Galicia, about local life, the competitive situation remains practically the same as in the past; that is yes, with less resources, and more precariously in many cases. But there's still a lot of shared competences where municipalities and joints coincide in different segments of the same competition, and where more than ever it takes to develop cooperation techniques. I want to stress that in Galicia that path is almost about to be made. The purpose of this motion is to mark some of these areas, the most perpetrators, and to invite most of the House to join, demanding a new way of cooperation with the local authorities in Greece for the benefit of the citizens. Opinion studies indicate that citizens do not know the distribution of competences between administrations, and add – adds this spokesman –: they do not need to know it, but they do fully identify the deficiencies in services. That is why in this motion we want to make clear those matters which belong to the superstructural debate, which are matters of political debate, but which are alien to social sensibilities; and that is why we will not talk about mergers of municipalities or forms of service delivery or models of cooperation. We want to propose four areas today where it is possible to reach agreed positions; areas that are sufficiently documented and where deficiencies have been the subject of multiple debates. It's time to move from the debate of theory to the resolution of theory. First of all, the transport of passengers. We have to reverse the decreasing trend. In Galicia last year, in the year 14, 10 million passengers were lost in public transport. It is necessary to overcome the current administrative vision which is based on the granting of lines – by course, extended until 2019 at least – in conditions strongly challenged by the National Competition Markets Commission, which, in a strong report, considers that the German standard, like other similar communities, can go against Community standards and, in any case, sets severe restrictions on competition in the interests of users. We believe that a transport policy must give satisfaction to three distinct problems: first, it must try to overcome the territorial limits of the municipalities and the artificial division between urban and interurban transport. The new residential reality of leisure and work has caused the dilution of the municipal borders in a diffuse urbanization habitat where hundreds of thousands of people live in the metropolitan crown of the main municipalities. The combined formulas are operating in other points in Spain and Europe, overcoming these restrictions. The U.S. metropolitan transport model does not address substantive issues and limits itself to subsidizing tariffs; a positive measure, of course, but does not respond to current needs, because metropolitan demands go through extending the services and frequencies of urban transport to its area of influence, whether residential, public services or leisure. It is not enough to modify tickets, but it is necessary to act on lines and frequencies, and the inertia of the Union is leading in this field to a high use of private transport; it is, of course, superior to other similar territories. Secondly, it is necessary to provide services to citizens where they need to go, to hospitals, not only in Vigo, unfortunately, where they have just discovered it these days, but also to business polygons, college campuses, large shopping areas or nightlife places, because they are places for people from very large geographic areas who today do not have regular services for travellers, being forced to make connections that involve waste of time and risk of safety at times, as happened with the deletion of the so-called "nightbus". Finally, we need to address the problem of current lines, frequencies and schedules to adapt them to the current user needs, because the ahead extension up to 2019 has not served up to now to move on that path. A second area of action we propose is housing policy, which has decreased by 80% since 2009 from 210 million euros to 41 million euros. The need for public promotion, public promotion housing, continues to exist, but the action of public authorities has been drastically reduced, while demand for housing has been maintained and market prices are rising by 5% in the last year. There's an urgent need to get back on track and develop housing plans with the Town Councils. We propose a third area of action in relation to waste policy. Galicia cannot continue to highlight the low percentages of recycling and use and the high rates of incineration with adverse effects on the environment and also with very high levels of rejection. We believe that it is time to address a new strategy with a greater degree of consensus and respect for Community directives. Finally, we propose to improve local funding. In these years with the Government of Greece, unconditional funding has been reduced by 12%, from 134 to 117 million euros, without other commitments such as the simplification and integration of the finalist grant lines, or the automatic transfer of funds corresponding to the agreed social services plan, permanent demands of the Fegamp and the municipalities. It is more, in the next motion, in the motion we are debating this morning, some further considerations have been introduced on the budgetary needs of the municipalities. I have just outlined four lines of action where inter-administrative cooperation is mandatory and where the dimension of problems does not allow any effective approach other than cooperation. Citizens refuse sterile confrontation between institutions and demand cooperation, without prejudice to the areas of political debate. The Socialist Party today presides over a hundred local corporations in Galicia, almost a third of the total. In his name, we propose to open a new stage aimed at solving the main problems of local Galician life. Without a doubt, the problems I have just announced are not the only ones, there are many others – rural development, demographic crisis, etc. – where they would also meet long-term agreements and strategies. But the four that I have just announced have a universal character, affect all the Greek municipalities in greater or lesser degrees. We believe that at the beginning of this new way of local corporations the political differences that we have expressed here often in the parliamentary debate should not prevent the timely agreements, and less should prevent the promotion of the necessary and indispensable actions for the welfare of citizens or to ensure the economic future of our councils. That's why we want to take advantage of this start of the mandate to invite all groups in the House, with their proposals, with their contributions, this debate, to start a new path together, asking the Government of the Union to be sensitive to these demands and to initiate a real policy of cooperation with the local world that has not existed until now in Galicia.

The municipalities are the level of proximity par excellence and therefore the most sensitive to the needs of citizenship. The source of institutional legitimization of the municipalities is to meet the needs of their neighbours, as well as to ensure a reasonable quality of life for all citizens. This can only be achieved through an effective articulation of a system for the distribution of competences which is clear, precise and fair, as well as through the configuration of a municipal financing system which can guarantee the exercise of these powers and adequate provision of social services at the local level. The council is a territorial political power, formed by the democratic principle, whose legitimacy and democratic quality is much greater than that of the deputies, conceived by the centralist scheme as the central government's direct delegations – this we know very well in the city of Ourense – to create a complex, client base, inter-administrative plot that destroys the social public sector and privatizes services. The second additional provision of Law 3/2012 allows not to apply collective agreements, agreements and agreements, and replaces collective bargaining with the simple right to information. The law is drawn up from an economic and financial perspective and thus makes it clear in the explanatory statement that the main objective of reform is to reduce the size of the local public sector. Then there is a radical cut in municipal competences and services, especially in education, health and social services, which will become exclusive competence of the Union at standard cost, that cost will be set unilaterally by the Central Administration without taking into account the distinguishing elements of our country: dispersed population, the weight of the rural environment, the rooting of the parishes. This centralist imposition, in addition to affecting the principle of subsidiarity – which can do the closest thing should not do the most distant thing – has important economic and operational consequences for the Union, by leaving without effect the local covenant signed in 2006, which regulates the shared support of the operating costs of municipal health and education centres. And you could start talking about how many debuggers don't work, or you could start talking about what's going on with the cyanobractories of the reservoirs that give the Arousa stream. Don't those affect the shellfish? Or what about the amount of pesticides that will also give to the Arousa stream? Doesn't that affect you either? What happens is that you don't have any work done to eliminate this issue, because they have a goal: to end the shellfish to install – I insist – large companies, especially from Norway and Japan, in the yellow rivers that expel our sailors. That's the goal of the People's Party. There's no other one!
